# Green Leaf Coding Review: Knowledge Base for a New Claude Session

Written 30 September 2026 from a working session with the user. Last updated the evening of 30 Sept after session 6 (SUST-REVENUE, Uganda hub KIIs).

**Start here next session:** read 11.1 (row procedure and strict reply format), then the conventions in 11.3, 12.2, 13.0, 15 (SUST checks), 16 (Ahmed's adopted rules) and 17.1. **Where to resume: the latest session section (44, REACH-ENGAGE, as of 8 Oct)**; conventions also in 18.0-31.0. Unfinished: ~13 ACC-DIRECT, all ACC-SHARED, unflagged ACC-SCHED, rest of ACC-OTHER, SUST-REVENUE, SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED, SUST-OTHER, SUST-CONTINUE. Log verdicts in the table of the current session section before replying. Appendix A holds the full Coding frame sheet verbatim, so the source docx and xlsx do not need re-reading. Purpose: let a fresh Claude session pick up the work with full context and without re-reading the source files.

Everything here comes from four sources: the user's Gmail (read-only), the two files the user attached (`GL Coding Framework_v0.3-10.docx` and `Green_Leaf_Coding_Frame.xlsx`, both in `C:\Users\bitac\OneDrive\Desktop\qwerty\`), the Guide page pasted by the user, and screenshots the user shared of the review app. Where something is inferred rather than seen, it says so.

---

## 1. Who the user is and what they want

- **User:** Chifundo Bita, **coding lead** on the Green Leaf Platforms Evaluation (Proportion Global team). Their sign-in role in the review app is "Coding lead".
- **What they are doing now:** starting their own review of the machine-suggested coded rows in Ahmed's web app.
- **How they want Claude to help:** the user pastes rows in the format `{code, text, Query, why this code}`. Claude checks whether the code suits the passage and either confirms or proposes a better code.
- **Verdict vocabulary (agreed with the user):** every verdict is one of **Accept, Reject, Recode, Pending**, followed by a short reason. Meanings:
  - **Accept:** the code fits; any fixes are cosmetic.
  - **Recode:** a different code, or an added second code, fits better. Name it.
  - **Reject:** the row should not be coded at all (interviewer speech, filler, not about the programme, excluded by the frame). Give a one-line reason, because the app asks for one.
  - **Pending:** cannot decide without something outside the row (audio check, translation, figure verification, identity check). Say what is needed.
- **Useful extras to ask for when rows are pasted:** session ID, line pointer, layer (TRN or SUM), whether the row is flagged (magenta), and the pass source (both passes, pass A only, pass B only).
- The user prefers concise, clear, step-by-step answers and asked to "fully understand, do not just skim" the source documents.

## 2. Project context

- **Evaluation:** Green Leaf Platforms Evaluation, Ghana and Uganda. **FRI = Farm Radio International**, the client. The programme is a farmer radio programme (locally Akuafo Shefa on Garden City Radio per the app's flag; whether "Akuafo Mrre" is the same programme or Garden City's separate Friday programme is unconfirmed, see section 14) with a phone-in and, in Uganda, the **Uliza** interactive platform.
- **Hubs:** Ghana has Radio Savannah, Garden City Radio and Volta Star. Uganda has BBS Radio (Masindi hub), Radio Pacis (Arua hub) and Ateker FM (Moroto hub).
- **Team (as named in the emails):**
  - **Chifundo Bita:** coding lead (the user).
  - **Ahmed Saalim Iddrisu:** built the review tool, codes Ghana.
  - **Mrignaina Tikku (Columbia):** report writer, leads analysis for the Case Study Publication.
  - **John:** coder for Uganda, supplies the Uganda transcripts and calibration sessions.
  - **Josephine Klein:** project manager (Proportion Global, Portugal).
  - **Thomas:** coder, also holds the business/ROI side and supplied API credits.
- **Key dates and plan (from Josephine's recap of 25 Sept):**
  - Client meeting 28 Sept, 2pm Lisbon, with John and Thomas on ROI/business viability.
  - Chifundo runs review from 25 Sept through Tue 29 Sept EOD, so analysis can start Wed 30 Sept.
  - Mrignaina drafts her sections and distributes the rest by Wed 30 Sept.
  - Everyone fills their sections by Fri 2 Oct so the draft can be handed to the client.
  - As of 30 Sept the review is only 4% done, so this schedule is at risk (see section 8).
- **Ahmed's status email of 28 Sept:** the tool is running on the full corpus, 74 sessions (33 Ghana, 41 Uganda) at that date. Ahmed and Chifundo hand-coded the three Ghana calibration sessions and agreed well on the short focus group and poorly on the two long interviews. The cause was working differences, not the frame. Twelve contested passages were resolved against the frame's own do-not-apply rules, two working conventions were adopted, and one frame change was made (REACH-ENGAGE now works in both sets). Ahmed's half was **not blind**, so that figure cannot be reported as inter-coder reliability. A clean number needs fresh material, and Fodome Xelu is the suggested candidate since Chifundo has not seen Ahmed's coding of it.
- **Data protection note:** the app holds verbatim farmer quotes behind a login limited to six named Google accounts. The team agreed this is acceptable.

## 3. The review app

- **URL:** https://greenleaf-review.netlify.app (Google sign-in with the whitelisted address; Google shows an "unverified app" warning that is expected).
- **Navigation tabs:** Home, Sessions, Review by code, Frame, Report, Stats, Progress, Guide, Search, Lead queue. A search box for transcripts sits in the header.
- **What the engine does:** reads each transcript twice, independently, against Coding Framework v0.3 and suggests rows (code, exact words, line pointer). Pass A sees the worked examples and the team's earlier decisions. Pass B sees the rules only. Before a row is shown, three mechanical checks run: the quote must be an exact match, the code must be one of the 62, and the line pointer must exist. Rows failing any check are discarded.
- **Nothing counts as coded until a person accepts it.** Findings are never produced by the tool.
- **Row tags:**
  - *both passes* / *pass A only* / *pass B only*
  - confidence *high* (clearly meets apply-when and no do-not-apply), *medium* (close to a sibling code), *low* (plausible but uncertain)
  - **magenta border = flagged**: the passage touches something the fieldwork already knows is wrong or doubtful (for example dollar figures that are really cedis, the Uliza claims at the Ghana hubs, the 8,000 farmers estimate with no stated method). Flagged rows can never be bulk-accepted.
  - *Quantified* (Yes/No) appears on ECO-COMPARATOR rows only.
  - COST and REACH rows carry a **figure block** (value, unit, currency, period, basis, who pays, confidence). Every block says "No, transcript only" until someone presses **Verify**, listens to the audio at the timestamp and records whether they heard the same figure, a different one, or could not tell.
- **Keyboard:** `J`/`K` move between rows, `A` accept, `R` reject (one-line reason required), `X` recode, `E` ask the lead, `N` note, `C` context, `U` undo, `F` one-at-a-time view. Row filters on a session page: all, pending, accepted, rejected, recoded. Views: List, One at a time.
- **Roles:**
  - *Coder:* review rows, add notes, add rows the engine missed, ask the lead.
  - *Coding lead (the user):* everything a coder does, plus the **Lead queue** (rows a reviewer asked about and rows where two blind reviewers disagreed; the lead's decision is final for the row), **closing sessions** (locks the rows; the report writer quotes only from closed sessions; can reopen), **choosing the double-coding sample** (open a session, press "Double-code this session"; about one in ten per country), watching the **Frame** page, and **adopting or dropping amendments** (only the lead can adopt).
  - *Report writer (Mrignaina):* sees everything, can decide rows, uses each code's Evidence page and the Report page.
  - *Project manager (Josephine):* Home is the six-stage pipeline (Awaiting transcript, Not yet coded, In review, With the lead, Reviewed, Closed); can export.
- **Safe bulk-accept:** rows that are both-passes, high-confidence and unflagged can be accepted together. Preview the list, untick any you want to read, then confirm.
- **Double-coded sessions:** two people decide each row without seeing each other; disagreements go to the lead queue.
- **Search:** finds every transcript line containing a phrase; lines with no code are passages the engine did not code (the lead decides whether it should have).
- **Export button** (Sessions page): an Excel file with Coding log, Quant extraction, Charting matrix, Convergence, Session register, Agreement and Frame amendments sheets; never overwrites anyone's working file.
- **What the tool never does:** write findings, decide anything in Section 11 of the framework, revise a hub tier, translate, quote from a summary form, or verify a figure against audio.
- **Sealed sessions:** two calibration sessions (one per country) are not to be reviewed until a tool version is formally accepted. Ahmed said he would tell the user which two. **Not confirmed** whether the tool enforces this or it is discipline only.
- **Progress page:** shows per person last active time, rows decided, notes, rows added and figures checked. It never shows accept/reject rates.

- **Stats:** both passes 68 accepted / 1,674 pending; pass A only 19 / 508; pass B only 17 / 576; every class 100% precision with 0 rejected and 0 recoded. Ghana 104 accepted, Uganda 0. No double-coding sample yet.
- **Progress:** 104 of 2,862 rows reviewed (4%); 104 decided this week; 1 of 5 active; 0 with the lead; 0 of 76 sessions closed. Projection at 52 rows/day finishes around 23 Nov.
- **Lead queue:** empty.

## 4. Settled team decisions (from the Guide, dated 26 Sept 2026, by Ahmed and Chifundo)

### How we code
1. **Code the answer, not the question.** An interviewer's question is not evidence of what is true. Where the question is needed to make the answer intelligible, point at the span covering both and say so in the query field. (Calibration round 1: nine rows on one side and four on the other quoted the interviewer.)
2. **Code the whole session at even density.** Work the transcript in order and code every passage meeting a rule, not one theme. (13 rows against 23 on the same interview, only two in common.)
3. **One line can carry several statements.** In a translated focus group a line is often a whole answer. Code each statement as its own row. (Sankpagla L23, 300 characters over five sentences.)

### Changes to the frame already in force (belong in the next framework version)
- **REACH-ENGAGE moved from the Hub set to Both.** A farmer phoning in, texting or asking a question on air is Green Leaf's main interaction mechanism and was landing in ACC-OTHER.
- **SUST-CONTINUE:** added to *do not apply*: money the station receives without a payer named. No payer named means not SUST-CONTINUE.
- **COST-SETUP:** added to *do not apply*: fuel, per diems and drivers for a routine field trip. Those recur, so they are COST-CASH.
- **ACC-SHARED:** added to *apply when*: the test is whether the person reached had heard it themselves. If not, ACC-SHARED. If everyone had, the onward talk is DIFF.

### Boundaries settled (each appears beside its code in the app)
| Passage (abridged) | Code | Not |
|---|---|---|
| "They open the phone lines during the programme for people to ask questions…" | ACC-OTHER (REACH-ENGAGE is better where available) | ACC-DIRECT (needs a set the respondent owns/controls; this is participation) |
| "Through our daily conversations and meetings." | ACC-SHARED (reaches people with no set) | DIFF-INFORMAL |
| "There was then the extension officer, Mr. Diklek." | CTX-HUB (someone else at the hub) | CTX-ROLE |
| "When EFU came, it increased because regional data was added to the hub." | CTX-HIST, second code CTX-HUB | CTX-HUB alone |
| "And there's a private TV station that's Rama TV." | FUNC-PARTNER (named as carrying the programme) | ECO-ACTOR |
| "…Something between 1,200, 1,300. Per hour." (station airtime rate quote) | SUST-DEMAND-STATED (a quoted rate card) | COST-INKIND |
| "The actual course… we go to the community to talk to the farmers first" | FUNC-CONTENT (how Green Leaf is produced, no other source in view) | ECO-COMPARATOR |
| "…we need a fuel, we need a driver, then you need… two staff." | COST-CASH (recurring field cost) | COST-SETUP |
| "We have 12,000 from the district." | SUST-CONTINUE (money received, payer named) | COST-CASH |
| "…because we don't have sponsorship, we come live and they repeat." | SUST-LAPSE | SUST-CONTINUE |
| "…does the program generate any sponsorship… revenue? No." | SUST-REVENUE (explicit absence is codeable; span question and answer, note it in the query) | n/a |

## 5. The Coding Framework v0.3 (10 September 2026)

### 5.1 Structure and rules
- **Purpose:** turn the Green Leaf fieldwork into evidence for the case study and ROI. A codebook form of thematic analysis (not reflexive): structured frame agreed in advance, IRR is not the definition of quality.
- **Parent document:** Research Plan (Section 7 Pre-Analysis Plan). Section references used: RP 4.1.1 hub tiers, 4.2/6 GESI and consent, 4.3 synthesis tools (Journey Mapping, Service Blueprinting, Business Model Canvas), 7.1 coding approach and 10% double coding, 7.2 convergence, 7.4 hub tier fixed.
- **Four client disaggregation commitments carried by codes:** cost per farmer reached for women and men separately, direct versus second-hand access reported both ways, Lens 3 modelled for female and male headed households, passive listening separated from active engagement.
- **Three layers per session (held in one folder):**
  - **AUD** audio: not coded directly; authority of last resort for any disputed figure, name or quote.
  - **TRN** English transcript: the primary coding layer and the **only layer from which a verbatim quote may be taken** (with AUD).
  - **SUM** structured summary form: used where no usable transcript exists and for every quantitative field; **never quoted verbatim**.
- **Three non-discretionary rules:** *Quotation* only from TRN or AUD. *Pointers*: every coded passage carries layer and position (transcript line, audio timestamp, or summary section and field). *Figures*: no figure enters the ROI workbook from a transcript alone; the transcription engine renders Ghana cedis as dollars and garbles numbers, so verify against audio or the note taker's written record and carry the verification status.
- **12 families, 62 codes, two standardised levels.** Anything more specific goes in the coding note and is not harmonised. Each family ends in an **OTHER** code (CTX-OTHER … GESI-OTHER): apply sparingly, never as a parking space for another family's material, every use reviewed fortnightly, recurring content promoted to a full code with only affected passages recoded.
- **Two working sets** (so no coder holds all 62):
  - **Community set** (IDI, FGD): ACC, KNOW, ADOPT, INC, DIFF, GESI = 29 codes.
  - **Hub set** (Hub KII, Field Observation, partner interviews): CTX, FUNC, COST, REACH, SUST, ECO = 33 codes.
  - GESI is available to hub coders. **REACH-ENGAGE now also works in the community set** (26 Sept change).
- **Naming:** codes written in full as FAMILY-CODE, never abbreviated or numbered. A passage may carry a second code where it genuinely says more than one thing.
- **Session attributes** (recorded once per session, never coded as themes): session_id (`Country_Instrument_Hub_Community_NN_Date`), country, hub, hub_tier (fixed, never revised by fieldwork, RP 7.4), exposure_observed (reported as a Journey Mapping finding, never a revised tier), community, instrument (Hub KII, IDI, FGD, Field Observation), respondent_category (SM, HC, EO, RC, EPC, RSR, Radio Presenter, Farmer, Group), respondent_sex, session_date, language, consent_status (On recording, Signed form, Community-level form to verify, Not documented), recording_status, transcript_status (Full, Partial, Unusable, Pending translation, None), data_quality_flag (Clean, Verify figures, Verify identity, Recover from audio, Do not use unqualified), participant_count (FGD only).

### 5.2 The 62 codes
Stage letters B to G follow the pathway (B awareness/access, C interpretation and trust, D discussion and trial, E adoption and drop-off, F income, G women's institutional roles); n/a for hub codes; "cross" for GESI cross-cutting. Definitions abridged from the workbook's Coding frame sheet; the "not" column is the do-not-apply boundary.

**CTX: Context, role and operating status (Hub set)**
| Code | Definition | Not (use instead) |
|---|---|---|
| CTX-ROLE | The respondent's own account of their role, tenure and who they answer to | Others' roles: CTX-HUB |
| CTX-HUB | Who makes up the hub, how the roster is assembled, turnover or vacancy | External partners: FUNC-PARTNER |
| CTX-STATUS | Whether each named element is fully active, partially active, changed or lapsed. Coded per element, never for the hub as a whole | Element that never existed |
| CTX-HIST | Prior phases, predecessor programmes, sequence of funders (DAASSP, FAO, ESRF, IKEA Foundation, AGRA/PIATA, CABI) | Current funding: SUST-CONTINUE |

**ACC: Access and exposure (Community set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| ACC-DIRECT | Respondent hears the programme on a radio or device they themselves own or control | Shared or someone else's set: ACC-SHARED |
| ACC-SHARED | Content reaches a person other than through a set they control: shared radio, group listening, community relay, or being told by someone else | Onward talk among people who all heard it: DIFF |
| ACC-BARRIER | Anything preventing or limiting exposure: no set, no power, no signal, cost, timing, language, competing obligation | Barriers to acting on advice: ADOPT-BARRIER |
| ACC-SCHED | Broadcast slot, repeats, changes including audience-driven ones | Content decisions: FUNC-CONTENT |

**KNOW: Knowledge, content and trust (Community set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| KNOW-RECALL | A named, datable piece of advice reproducible with enough detail to check against a broadcast | General appreciation: KNOW-VALUE |
| KNOW-TRUST-PROCESS | Credibility from how the programme is made: research, briefing, editorial discipline, reputational stake | Trust from results: KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME |
| KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME | Credibility from the advice having worked, or the programme being consistently there | Reasoning about production |
| KNOW-VALUE | Positive or negative appraisal with no recallable content | Never evidence of knowledge gain or behaviour change |
| KNOW-CONFUSE | Content the respondent could not act on, did not understand, or did not believe | Clear but unaffordable advice: ADOPT-BARRIER |

**ADOPT: Practice change (Community set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| ADOPT-TRIAL | A specific practice started, stopped or altered with the programme given as the reason (both practice and attribution needed) | Either missing |
| ADOPT-SUSTAIN | A tried practice still in use at interview (across at least one season) | Single trial, no continuation |
| ADOPT-DROP | A practice tried then abandoned, with reason where given | Do not infer from silence |
| ADOPT-BARRIER | Anything preventing action on advice heard and understood: cost, inputs, land, labour, water, market, permission | Obstacles to hearing: ACC-BARRIER |

**INC: Income, investment and return (Community set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| INC-CHANGE | Change in yield, income, cost or post-harvest loss attributed to a practice from the programme | Income change unlinked to the programme |
| INC-INVEST | How the first application of a practice was paid for and where resulting income went | Programme costs: COST |
| INC-MARKET | Constraints converting production into income: buyers, prices, transport, storage, timing | Production constraints: ADOPT-BARRIER |

**DIFF: Diffusion and social transmission (Community set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| DIFF-FORMAL | Passes through a standing group with membership and meetings (association, cooperative, savings group) | Ad hoc gatherings |
| DIFF-KIN | Passes within household or extended family | Neighbours |
| DIFF-INFORMAL | Passes through unstructured neighbour conversation or communal gathering points | Where a named group is the vehicle |

**FUNC: Platform functioning and governance (Hub set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| FUNC-CONTENT | How topics are chosen, scripted, scheduled, prepared; planning assets | Slot timing: ACC-SCHED |
| FUNC-FEEDBACK | How farmer responses are collected, recorded, acted on | Platform engagement counts: REACH-ENGAGE |
| FUNC-PARTNER | Organisations outside the hub contributing content, staff, airtime, funding or reach | Hub members: CTX-HUB |
| FUNC-GOV | Who decides, who is accountable, how disputes or resourcing gaps are resolved | Who does the work: CTX-HUB |

**COST: Cost and resourcing (Hub set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| COST-CASH | Money actually paid for an input: fees, airtime, per diems, fuel, materials, equipment | Donated or waived items: COST-INKIND |
| COST-INKIND | A resource supplied by an organisation without cash: donated airtime, government staff time, offices, equipment, simulcast (replacement cost where a basis exists) | Individuals absorbing personal cost: COST-VOLUNTEER |
| COST-VOLUNTEER | Time, travel or materials absorbed by an individual, not an organisation | Do not merge with institutional in-kind |
| COST-SETUP | Costs to start or re-start a hub: equipment, initial training, installation (one-off, with year) | Recurring costs, including routine field-trip fuel and per diems |
| COST-BOUNDARY | A cost the respondent is aware of but does not own: country coordination, licensing, overhead | Do not enter in the hub cost table; flag for the FRI cost request |

**REACH: Reach, output and engagement (Hub set; REACH-ENGAGE also Community)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| REACH-AUDIENCE | Any estimate of how many people hear the programme, with its basis | Do not record a figure without basis and population |
| REACH-ENGAGE | Counts of people who did something: called in, beeped, responded to a poll, attended | Do not conflate with audience size |
| REACH-PLATFORM | Uganda only. Figures from the Uliza platform, not recall | Never a complete audience measure; Uliza records connected call-backs only, no calls-attempted denominator |
| REACH-EPISODE | Episodes broadcast in a period, average episode length | Audience figures |
| REACH-ABSENT | Explicit statement that the respondent has no reach figure or the platform cannot supply one | Record as a positive finding, never a blank |

**SUST: Sustainability, revenue and continuation (Hub set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| SUST-CONTINUE | An element still running with the payer named (government, station, another donor, sponsor, community, unpaid time). Coded per element | No payer named: not this code |
| SUST-LAPSE | An element that has ceased or shrunk since support diminished, with what was lost | Never existed at that hub |
| SUST-REVENUE | Money the programme or station actually receives: sponsorship, advertising, paid airtime, donations (advertising kept separate from donation) | Hypothetical or rate-card pricing: SUST-DEMAND-STATED |
| SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED | Someone approached the hub or station wanting to sponsor, buy airtime on or redeploy a Green Leaf type programme, with money or a concrete offer | Hypothetical willingness to pay |
| SUST-DEMAND-STATED | Hypothetical pricing, rate cards, expressed interest without a transaction | Never report alongside revealed demand without stating the distinction |
| SUST-RESTART | What it would take to restore a lapsed element and what need not be spent again | Current running costs |

**ECO: Ecosystem positioning and comparators (Hub set)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| ECO-COMPARATOR | Any other way farmers get agricultural information or extension support, set against Green Leaf explicitly or by implication. Cost, coverage or efficiency information recorded where given but not required. Carries a **Quantified** field: Yes (cost/coverage/efficiency attached, enters ROI Lens 2 arithmetic) or No (Objective 4 positioning only) | Descriptions of Green Leaf alone |
| ECO-POLICY | Alignment with, or absence from, national extension or digital agriculture strategy | Hub-level governance: FUNC-GOV |
| ECO-ACTOR | Another organisation operating in the same space, named by a respondent | Partners of this hub: FUNC-PARTNER |

**GESI: Gender equality and social inclusion (both sets)**
| Code | Definition | Not |
|---|---|---|
| GESI-ACCESS | Any account of one group having more or less access to the broadcast, inputs or extension | Outcome differences with no access mechanism |
| GESI-NORM | A belief or convention shaping who may listen, speak, attend or adopt | Logistical barriers |
| GESI-ROLE | Ghana only (stage G). Presence, absence or effectiveness of women's agricultural roles in the hub, including WIAD and WIADO | Farmer-level gender findings |
| GESI-GAP | A design commitment on inclusion not met in the field | Substantive findings about gender |

**Six codes most likely to be applied inconsistently:** ACC-SHARED, KNOW-TRUST-PROCESS, KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME, COST-INKIND, COST-VOLUNTEER, SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED.

### 5.3 Turning codes into numbers (COST and REACH)
Every COST or REACH passage carries a block: `value` (as stated, not rounded), `unit`, `currency`, `reference_year` (year the figure refers to), `period`, `basis` (per session, episode, hour, trip, month, year, total), `funding_source` (Donor, Station-own, Government in-kind, Community, Personal, Other, Unknown), `confidence` (Measured, Estimate, Recalled, Not held), `source_layer`, `verified` (Yes against audio, No transcript only, Disputed), and `denominator_note` (REACH only). A code without these fields is incomplete and does not enter the workbook. Four rules:
1. **Never average a disagreement.** Radio Savannah: producer said GHS 1,500 per hour airtime; extension officer said GHS 1,200 to 1,300. Record both with respondents attached.
2. **A reach figure without a denominator note is not recorded.** "Approximately 8,000 farmers" is not a datum until it states 8,000 of what, measured how.
3. **Absence is data.** Record REACH-ABSENT or "not held" rather than leaving a blank.
4. **A comparison without figures is still coded**, with Quantified = No.

### 5.4 When a code becomes a finding (Section 8)
Coding gives coded passages, not findings. Every persona, blueprint stage or canvas cell must trace to convergent evidence from at least two instrument types (IDI against FGD from the same community, both against Field Observation from the same hub, and all three against Hub KII). Hub KII versus farmer-level disagreement is reported as a finding in its own right. **Current evidence base does not meet this everywhere:** five of ten Ghana communities have only one community-level instrument (Golinga, Bekwai, Tamale Metropolitan have IDIs but no FGD; Sakogu and Atwima Nerebihi have an FGD but no IDIs); Ghana recorded two field observations against seven scheduled and six Hub KIIs against eleven. Proposed: assess convergence at hub level for Ghana and record it as a documented deviation.

### 5.5 Coding procedure (Section 9)
1. Calibrate: three sessions per country coded independently by two people; amend and date the frame. 2. Code: one coder per session, instrument-matched set; attributes first, then codes, then quant block. 3. Double code 10% of each country's transcripts, sampled **at random**, by the other country's lead. 4. Reconcile: disagreements resolved by discussion and logged; where the rule changes, amend and increment the version. 5. Review OTHER codes fortnightly. 6. Chart: one row per session, one column per code, attributes as filters. Agreement is reported **per code and per country**, never pooled; where a code performs poorly, rewrite its rules rather than train coders into the ambiguity.

### 5.6 Constraints carried into coding (Section 10)
Currency rendering (cedis as dollars); local-language loss (Dagbani, Ewe, Runyoro, Lugbara, Karamojong not carried by the English transcript, so flag for translator recovery; nothing scheduled until a translation has a named owner and date; Moroto is the only Uganda hub not yet transcribed); partial transcripts and recordings (Fodome Xelu FGD transcript stops after ~10 of 19 minutes; second half of Kumasi MoFA interview unusable; one BBS Radio interview captured ~20%); identity conflicts (one Volta Star respondent named three ways: resolve from the consent form first); missing participant counts; consent taken at community level (verify signed forms before publishing any quote); respondent category (Ghana coded presenters and producers as Station Manager, Uganda coded actual station managers: do not pool hub-level codes across countries until settled); transcript handling procedure not yet documented; gender composition (fifteen-fifteen target missed; apply GESI-GAP; in the one women-only FGD men stood within listening distance); airtime treatment (where the station bears airtime cost, code airtime COST-INKIND at replacement cost, not COST-CASH, and do not build the Uganda cost base on the Ghana arrangement); tracker divergence (work from the session register).

### 5.7 Decisions required (Section 11) and proposed positions
1. Station Manager: keep as thin parent meaning hub radio programme lead; record actual role in the attribute. 2. Convergence at hub level for Ghana, as a documented deviation. 3. Reach denominator: a one-page definition per country, signed off; no cost-per-farmer figure published until it exists. 4. Authoritative tracker: the coding workbook's session register, reconciled once and checked on every update; needs Mrignaina's agreement because an upload figure has already been shown to the client. 5. Summary-form field codes: map, don't replace (Crosswalk sheet). 6. Definitional authority over a contested code: the research lead; amendments dated and versioned.

### 5.8 Annex: crosswalk and method basis
Two numbering systems exist: instrument codes (ROI-Cost-2, CTX-1) and summary-form codes (B1 to B8, C1 to C4, G1). The Crosswalk sheet maps 21 of them. Method sources cited: Bernard and Ryan via Saldaña (codebook fields), Fereday and Muir-Cochrane 2006 (a priori plus emergent), Gale et al. 2013 (framework method, OTHER codes), Braun and Clarke 2021 (codebook versus reflexive), O'Connor and Joffe 2020 (agreement per code), Zavrnik et al. 2024 (two standardised levels), Kowalski et al. 2024 and Nevedal et al. 2021 (coding from summaries with pointers), Cronin et al. 2023 and Dopp et al. 2019 (cost structure, reach denominator).

### 5.9 Embedded in the docx
A "Team Review" section holds Ahmed's proposal for the tool (to Chifundo) and Mrignaina's reply, plus 23 Google Docs comments. Points from the comments: the Crosswalk sheet does exist (Chifundo's earlier mistake in what he sent), the workbook has seven sheets; ECO-COMPARATOR loosened in v0.3; the Sankpagla FGD is proposed for calibration but cannot be coded until translated; the translation has no owner; Josephine asked that geography and audience be disaggregable in the log.

Sheets and content:
- **README:** what the workbook is, conventions, "Changes in 0.3" (ECO-COMPARATOR, airtime, GESI, revenue vs donation, register). Code count formula `COUNTA('Coding frame'!B2:B200)` gives 62.
- **Coding frame:** 62 rows with Family, Code, Label, Stage, Objective/RQ, Country, Definition, Apply when, Do not apply to, Example from the record, Source, Feeds, Set.
- **Case attributes:** the session attribute list above with examples.
- **Session register (96 rows):** Ghana 36 sessions plus 2 ecosystem interviews (Farmerline and Esoko, names to confirm), Uganda 47. Coding columns (transcript status, layer used, coded by, double-coded by, quality flag) are **empty**. Notes record: shared tracker shows 37 Ghana sessions with a Garden City field observation lacking a session row; three Uganda Radio Pacis rows dated 28-Sep are almost certainly 28-Aug; upload status blank for every Uganda row and N for seven Ghana rows (already shown to the client, needs Mrignaina's agreement to revise); the Moroto record says Uliza is active and, in the same sentence, that farmers do not know it (verify against audio; REACH-PLATFORM is Uganda only).
- **Coding log tabs:** "Ghana -Chifundo" (89 coded rows plus a stray empty row, dated 16 to 22 Sept), "Ghana - Ahmed" (97 rows, 24 Sept), "Uganda-John" (997 rows). Columns: Entry, Session ID, Layer, Pointer, Family, Code, Second code, Quantified, Passage or paraphrase, Coder, Date, Query. These are the hand-coded calibration logs, mostly Radio Savannah SM and EO KIIs and the Sankpagla FGD (Ahmed's also has part of Fodome Xelu).
- **Quant extraction:** three worked example rows (resource-person fee GHS 250 per session; airtime GHS 1,250 per hour with range 1,200 to 1,300 and producer's 1,500; 8,000 farmers per broadcast).
- **Crosswalk (21 rows):** instrument code to summary-form code to codebook code with status. Unverified labels: ROI-Cost-5, ROI-Reach-2, ROI-Reach-3. Blocked: ROI-Reach-4 (Uliza series dates outstanding from FRI). ROI-PSC-7 is assigned to two different questions. No instrument equivalent for B4 to B8 (per diems and field travel), C1 to C3 (sessions per year, slot, repeats), G1 (field trip versus studio cost contrast).

The app labels it TRN, but it is not verbatim participant speech. Whether such reconstructions are quotable is a Section 11 call and belongs to the lead. 10. **Sealed sessions and closing rules are unconfirmed:** ask Ahmed which two sessions are sealed, whether the tool blocks opening them, and whether a session with open double-coded disagreements can be closed.

ACC-BARRIER needs a stated obstacle, and ACC-DIRECT/ACC-SHARED need a listening mechanism. If audio confirms it is about the programme, Recode to KNOW-TRUST-OUTCOME; if it is generic radio, Reject as not about the programme. ## 9. Step-by-step review procedure agreed with the user

**Before the first row**
1. Ask Ahmed which two sessions are sealed and do not open them.
2. Choose the double-coding sample **before** reviewing anything (about one in ten per country, roughly 3 to 4 Ghana and 4 Uganda sessions), at random, not for convenience. Open the session and press "Double-code this session". Reviewing first makes the verdict non-blind. Claude can draw the random sample if given the session list.

**Reviewing**
3. Open Review by code and press Continue ("Continue, any country" to include Uganda). Codes open in family order.
4. For each row read the rule (definition, apply when, do not apply to), then the quoted passage, then context (`C`) if unclear, then any magenta flag note.
5. Check: is it the respondent's answer and not the interviewer's question; does it meet apply-when and avoid every do-not-apply; is the second code right; for COST or REACH is the figure block complete and the currency plausible.
6. Decide: `A` accept, `X` recode (choose a code or add a second), `R` reject (one-line reason), `E` ask the lead (rarely, since the user is the lead), `N` note. Leave Pending if audio or translation is needed.
7. Do not accept flagged rows on reflex; read the note. Keep figures "No, transcript only" until someone listens and presses Verify.

**Bulk-accept safely**
8. Review 20 to 30 rows of a code individually first. If almost all are right, preview the safe-button list (both passes, high confidence, unflagged), untick anything to read, spot-check about one in ten, then confirm.
9. Do not bulk-accept codes with fuzzy boundaries (ACC-DIRECT, ACC-OTHER).

**Keeping the frame honest**
10. If a code keeps causing doubt, propose a rewrite on the Frame page (30% rejection or more is the app's own threshold). Only the lead adopts amendments.
11. On a session page, check the unhighlighted stretches of the transcript (what the engine did not code) and decide whether any should have been.
12. Close a session when every row is decided and the lead queue is empty. Closing unlocks it for the report writer. Review by code is faster, but sessions only close when all their rows are done, so plan a sweep for leftovers.

## 11. Review session 1 log (30 Sept 2026): ACC-BARRIER, Uganda rows

The user reviewed ACC-BARRIER rows (Review by code, all countries) one screenshot at a time. Session 2 (ACC-DIRECT) is in section 12, which says where to resume. The ACC-DIRECT page in the app carries one settled decision: phone lines opened during the programme for questions is ACC-OTHER, not ACC-DIRECT (participation, not access; REACH-ENGAGE where counts exist).

### 11.1 How the user wants each row handled (required)
1. Read the row **in context** (surrounding lines, which question the answer really responds to, speaker tags).
2. Read the engine's **Query** and **Why this code**.
3. Check the code and its close siblings against **Appendix A** (the full `Coding frame` sheet, copied verbatim: definition, apply when, do not apply to, example) and the framework rules in section 5. **The user does not want the source files re-read from scratch each session**: Appendix A and section 5 replace them. Open the docx or xlsx only for something neither covers, and then only the relevant part.
4. Apply the Guide decisions (section 4) and the conventions below.
5. **Reply format (strict; the user corrected it twice):** the verdict line, then ONE quote block holding the note, and nothing else. The note opens by restating the decision in past tense. Example:
   `Recode to KNOW-CONFUSE.`
   `> Recoded to KNOW-CONFUSE. Unlike Olevu L59, she has used Uliza. Her problem is understanding the information… so John should check the audio before it's quoted.`
   Other openers: `Accept, as ACC-DIRECT.` / `> Accepted as ACC-DIRECT.`; `Reject.` / `> Rejected: <reason>.` No "What I checked", no confidence paragraph, no alternatives outside the note, and **no trailing line such as "Logged."** Do any file logging before writing the reply. Questions from the user (e.g. "what is ACC-DIRECT", "are you sure") get a normal short answer, not this format.
6. **Note style:** short, human, first-person-lead voice, same content as before: the reason, plus any missed code on the same lines ("…is ACC-DIRECT and needs coding if it isn't already"), missing speaker tags, quoting cautions.
6b. **Short notes, no confidence figures (user, 2 Oct):** never write "about 70% sure" in a note; 1-2 sentences, reason plus only the essential missed code or caution.
6a. **Confidence floor (user, 1 Oct):** only give Accept/Recode/Reject when at least 70% sure, after reading the lines around the highlight and testing the nearest sibling codes. Below that, Pending with what would settle it.
7. If the user asks "are you sure?", give an honest confidence answer: what is solid, what alternative reading exists, what would change it. Short.

### 11.2 App constraints found
- Notes **cannot be deleted**, only added. If a verdict changes, the new note says so ("Changing my earlier pending call to Accept…").
- ~~Rows cannot be added~~ **Updated 30 Sept (later):** the app now has **"Add a missing row"** and **"Don't quote"** buttons on each row, and in the lead queue a **"Make this a rule"** option after deciding (sends the ruling to the Frame page as a proposal). Missed codes flagged in earlier notes (11.5, 12.5 and the section 13-15 tables) can now be added as rows; use "Don't quote" instead of writing "not quotable" in notes.
- The Recode box has **one dropdown** (appears to replace, not add, a code). If a row needs its code kept plus a second one, Accept and put the second code in the note.
- **Audio checks:** do not assign to Mrignaina (no time). Uganda audio: **John**. Ghana: **Ahmed**. Only ask for an audio check where a quote or figure actually depends on it; one-word answers are simply marked "not quotable".

### 11.3 Conventions settled this session (apply consistently)
- **Barriers to using Uliza or getting through on the phone-in → ACC-OTHER**, not ACC-BARRIER (ACC-BARRIER is obstacles to *hearing*; REACH-ENGAGE needs counts; FUNC-FEEDBACK is Hub set). Framework Section 1 commits to separating passive listening from active engagement, and no code covers barriers to engagement, so this is a gap worth a Frame amendment. Open alternative the lead could adopt instead: treat Uliza call-backs as a content channel, which would send these back to ACC-BARRIER. Decide once for all Uliza rows.
- **Exception:** a respondent who *has used* Uliza but cannot understand the information delivered → **KNOW-CONFUSE** (Rondo_02 L75).
- **Non-listening with no obstacle named → ACC-OTHER** ("none", "not taken time", "not ardent listeners"). **Lack of time stated as the reason → ACC-BARRIER** ("rarely get time").
- **Cause vs effect:** "No" to "do you have a radio or phone" = no set = ACC-BARRIER. "None" to "how often do you listen" = effect only = ACC-OTHER.
- **Limiting counts as well as preventing** (definition says "preventing or limiting"): a competing preference (sports), broken radio while still listening on a phone, busy days cutting frequency, all ACC-BARRIER.
- **Owner controls the radio** (answer to the control probe) → ACC-BARRIER (limits non-owners); no GESI-ACCESS unless sex or group stated.
- **Age groups count as a "group" under GESI-ACCESS** (the L35 probe itself names "older farmers").
- **Personal belief or bias is not GESI-NORM** (needs a belief or convention shaping who may participate, i.e. a community norm): Kahaara L42 bias against messenger; Rupa_05 L75 "I thought it's for the rich".
- **Family radio the respondent mainly controls → ACC-DIRECT** ("own or control"). **Shared household set with turn-taking → ACC-SHARED**, plus GESI-ACCESS where one sex has less say (Kahaara L56).
- **Same barrier repeated in answer to different questions → keep separate rows**, do not merge.
- Topics named without detail (intercropping, agroforestry, kitchen gardens) are **too general for KNOW-RECALL**.
- Guide test for ACC-SHARED vs DIFF applied: cooperative chairperson advising someone who has not heard the programme → ACC-SHARED.

## 12. Review session 2 log (30 Sept 2026): ACC-DIRECT, Uganda rows

The user reviewed ACC-DIRECT rows (Review by code, all countries). At the start the page read **32 of 82 decided, 50 left**. This session gave 37 verdicts (table 12.4), so roughly **13 ACC-DIRECT rows remain**; confirm in the app. The last row reviewed was **UG_IDI_RadioPacis_Rondo_02_28Aug L75**. **Next session: resume ACC-DIRECT from the next pending row, then ACC-SHARED.** The rows came in session order (Uganda FGDs, then IDIs by hub and community). One row (Kyakamese_05 L31-L33) was shown twice; same verdict both times.

### 12.1 Working method confirmed this session
- The row-handling steps and the strict reply format are in 11.1 (verdict line + one quote-block note, nothing else).
- Verdicts are logged in this file as they are given (table 12.4); do the logging before the reply.
- The user asked plain questions twice ("what is ACC-DIRECT and why not here", "are you sure"): answer those in normal short prose, stating what is solid, the alternative reading, and what would change the call.

### 12.2 Conventions settled for ACC-DIRECT (apply consistently)
- **ACC-DIRECT needs two things: a set the respondent owns or controls, and the programme heard on it.** A set with no listening → **Reject** (Kyakamese_05 L31-L33; his non-listening already sits in the L25 ACC-OTHER row). Note: the xlsx apply-when ("ownership or routine personal use") read literally would accept ownership alone; we follow the definition because ACC-DIRECT feeds the direct arm of the reach denominator. If the lead ever decides otherwise, apply it to every "has a set but doesn't listen" row.
- **Radio named in answer to the general "where do farmers get information" question (A-module, before Green Leaf is raised), or radio set against other sources → ECO-COMPARATOR (Quantified: No)**, even in community sessions (Naitakwai L10, Olevu L10, Tilevu_02 L13; same as Atwima L14). ECO-COMPARATOR is a Hub-set code; the alternative the lead could choose is Reject ("radio in general, not the programme"). Named companies or NGOs in such lines → ECO-ACTOR.
- **B1 answers ("when did you first hear about Green Leaf") with no set stated → ACC-OTHER** (awareness; the frame has no awareness code, a known gap). The ACC-DIRECT goes on the B2 line where the set is stated, so the person is not counted twice (Rupa_06 L17, Kyamugweri_01 L17, Kyamugweri_06 L29/L31, Labongo_02 L20, Andifeku_04 L16, Andifeku_05 L16, Rondo_01 L16). **Exception:** a B1 line that itself names the respondent's own device ("on the radio using my phone every Friday") → ACC-DIRECT (Andifeku_06 L16).
- **First heard through a person or group, before hearing it themselves → ACC-SHARED** (Andifeku_07 L16 farmers' group; Guide test). **Being told when programmes air, not the content → ACC-OTHER**, not ACC-SHARED (Labongo_02 L20, Muruli Moses).
- **B3 frequency answers on a set shown elsewhere to be theirs → ACC-DIRECT, kept as a separate row** (different question) (Komaret_03 L23, Labongo_02 L26, Andifeku_03 L27). The same applies to a repeat of ownership in answer to a different probe (Kyamugweri_06 L35 and L37).
- **One-word answers to the control probe ("yes, I have the control") → ACC-DIRECT, not quotable alone; span question + answer if quoted** (Kahaara L58). A facilitator line inside a span is fine when the answer makes no sense without it (Labongo_03 L34-L36).
- **Household or family radio:** respondent decides or puts the programme first → ACC-DIRECT (Labongo_03, Andifeku_03 L24, Labongo_02 L23). Respondent owns it and shares with a spouse who does not limit them → ACC-DIRECT (Kyamugweri_06). **Set owned and controlled by the spouse → ACC-SHARED**, and the control line gets ACC-BARRIER + GESI-ACCESS (Andifeku_07 L16/L19, where the wife controls it; Kahaara L63, where the husband does).
- **Past own set (before it broke) + listened to the programme → ACC-DIRECT**; the current device is a separate ACC-DIRECT row (Komaret_01 L16 two rows; Andifeku_05 L22).
- **Switching to one's own phone when others have the radio, no listening lost → ACC-DIRECT only, no ACC-BARRIER** (Kyamugweri_06 L39). Contrast Komaret_01, where the broken radio could not be replaced (ACC-BARRIER).
- **Holding station phone numbers, or having used Uliza → ACC-OTHER** (participation, not hearing); not REACH-ENGAGE unless counts are given (Labongo_02 L94, Rondo_02 L75).
- **GESI-ACCESS needs a stated difference** (xlsx apply-when: "stated differences in access, ownership or entitlement"). A woman saying her husband does not limit her is not coded (Kyamugweri_06 L37: recoded to ACC-DIRECT, GESI dropped); raised as a Frame question (11.6).
- **KNOW family:** a topic list describing the programme is not KNOW-VALUE (needs an appraisal); line planting, vaccination of livestock, kitchen gardens, watering are too general for KNOW-RECALL. "Not yet practising" with no reason is not ADOPT-BARRIER.

### 12.3 Recode dropdown handling
- Where an engine row carried a second code that fails (ACC-DIRECT + GESI-ACCESS at Kyamugweri_06 L37), Recode to the single code that stands and say in the note which code was dropped and why.

## 13. Review session 3 log (30 Sept 2026): ACC-OTHER

The user switched to ACC-OTHER before finishing ACC-DIRECT (about 13 ACC-DIRECT rows still pending) and ACC-SHARED. At the start the page read **5 of 88 decided, 83 left** (accepted 5, recoded 0, rejected 0). The app warned that **John Mutebe is on this code at the same time** and suggested a different country filter so the same rows are not decided twice.

### 13.0 Conventions settled for ACC-OTHER
- **Farmer proposals for a new relay route** (information centres, pen drives, extension officers) → ACC-OTHER: not ACC-SHARED (not in use) and not ACC-SCHED (not a station slot). A request to change the airing time → ACC-SCHED.
- **Exhortations to peers** ("always keep a radio close") → Reject, no access fact.
- **Stated absence of an access gap** ("nobody misses it") → ACC-OTHER as a recorded absence, not ACC-BARRIER (Fodome Xelu L72-L73).
- **Other TV or radio agric programmes** → ECO-COMPARATOR, Quantified No (Sakogu L13, Sankpagla L51).

## 14. Review session 4 log (30 Sept 2026): ACC-SCHED, flagged rows

After the ACC-OTHER rows above (Ghana, about 20 verdicts; remaining ACC-OTHER count not confirmed, and John Mutebe was working the same code), the user moved to ACC-SCHED with the **flagged only** filter: 2 of 2 rows pending. Unflagged ACC-SCHED rows (34 at the start of the day) still to do.

Reminders for ACC-SCHED: slot times, day of week, repeats, clashes, slot moves, audience-driven changes; not content (FUNC-CONTENT). A request to change the airing time → ACC-SCHED; a proposed relay/playback at information centres → ACC-OTHER (13.0). Day/time stated alongside listening can carry ACC-SCHED as well as ACC-DIRECT. Flagged rows: read the flag, check slot claims against the hub's known slot (Ahmed for Ghana, John for Uganda).

**Open check for Ahmed (blocks several Garden City rows):** is "Akuafo Mrre" Green Leaf or Garden City's separate Friday farmers' programme? The flag names Green Leaf at this hub as **Akuafo Shefa**; Jamasi L18 has Akuafo Mrre every Friday (matches the separate programme); Bekwai_01 L8 has Akuafo Mrre on Sunday on Dess/Best FM, which the RA called the same broadcast. Section 2's "Akuafo Shefa / Akuafo Mrre" was an assumption, not confirmed. Rows waiting on this: Jamasi L18, L20; affects the Bekwai rows.

## 15. Review session 5 log (30 Sept 2026): SUST-REVENUE

The user left ACC-SCHED part-way (flagged Jamasi rows Pending on the Akuafo Mrre check; unflagged rows still to do; advised against the 16-row safe bulk-accept until 20-30 rows are reviewed individually) and moved to SUST-REVENUE: **70 of 70 pending**, 15 "safe" rows offered (same advice: do not bulk-accept yet).

Checks for SUST-REVENUE rows (Hub set, Appendix A + Guide):
- Needs **money actually received** by the programme or station: sponsorship, advertising, paid airtime, donations. Keep **advertising separate from donation**; businesses paying to be associated with the programme = advertising (Thomas, 9 Sept).
- Rate cards, quoted prices, hypothetical interest → SUST-DEMAND-STATED. An approach or concrete offer → SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED.
- **Explicit "no revenue" is codeable absence** (Guide); span question + answer and say so in the query.
- Boundary with SUST-CONTINUE: an element kept running with a named payer (government budget, district money, station's own budget, unpaid time) → SUST-CONTINUE ("We have 12,000 from the district" = SUST-CONTINUE, Guide). No payer named → not SUST-CONTINUE.
- Station revenue unconnected to Green Leaf (Volta Star: Feed Ghana airtime, input-dealer ads) is still coded, with the separation stated.
- Figures: cedis may be rendered as dollars; no figure into the workbook from transcript alone (Ahmed for Ghana audio, John for Uganda).

## 16. Ahmed's frame update (email pasted 30 Sept 2026, after 259 decisions)

Ahmed turned 31 of the user's 34 changes into four rules written into the frame (both engine passes see them; shown beside the code): (1) call-in/Uliza barriers → ACC-OTHER, REACH-ENGAGE with counts; (2) no reason, no barrier → ACC-OTHER; (3) first-heard answers → ACC-OTHER unless the line says whose set; (4) ACC-DIRECT needs ownership "on the line", radio as one source among others → ECO-COMPARATOR. **Needs the lead's yes:** ECO-COMPARATOR moved into both sets (was Hub only; six recodes flagged out of set). Eleven recodes became engine worked examples (incl. Kahaara L89, Komaret_03 L78, Olevu L10). Re-running the 49 farmer sessions; only undecided rows replaced. New app features: "Don't quote" (reason shown to Mrignaina), "Add a missing row", "Ask the lead" names the codes in question + "Make this a rule", bulk accept off in Uganda for ACC-BARRIER and ACC-DIRECT. He flagged ECO-COMPARATOR picking up "I prefer meetings to farm visits" in the Kahaara re-run (DIFF?).

Reply points drafted with the user: rule 4 "on that line" is stricter than our convention (ownership stated elsewhere in the same interview counts: Komaret_03 L23, Labongo_02 L26, Andifeku_03 L27) and ACC-DIRECT also needs listening (Kyakamese_05 reject); rule 2 needs the time boundary (busy/farm work/no time → ACC-BARRIER); rule 3 needs the ACC-SHARED exception (first heard through a person or group) and the Golinga_02 predecessor-programme ruling; rule 1 needs the KNOW-CONFUSE exception (used Uliza, can't understand) and the Golinga_04 phone-in-segment ruling; ECO-COMPARATOR in both sets: yes, and ECO-ACTOR likely needs the same (used on Olevu L10, Sakogu, Bekwai); ask whether Pending rows with notes count as "decided" in the re-run.

**Ahmed's reply (same day):** all four tweaks adopted (KNOW-CONFUSE for used-Uliza-can't-follow; named time/work reason stays ACC-BARRIER; first heard through a person or group = ACC-SHARED; ACC-DIRECT set can be stated anywhere in the interview and the person must actually listen). **ECO-COMPARATOR and ECO-ACTOR now in both sets.** Shown on the Guide and beside each code. Re-run only replaces rows nobody has touched: a row is kept if it has a note, any decision (incl. reopened or awaiting a second reviewer), was sent to the lead, or was added by hand. Opened-but-undecided rows with no note are replaced. Sessions re-run in the morning are re-run again with the revised wording, finishing the evening of 30 Sept; if a row disappears mid-review, reload. **Not answered yet:** Akuafo Mrre vs Akuafo Shefa, which two sessions are sealed, "Not recorded" sessions with timestamps. (These three were not in the email thread; they still need asking separately.) Details in the emails not recorded above: Ahmed's rule 1 listed the cases (no number, lines busy, can't catch the number); he checked that Kahaara L87-L89 now come out as ACC-OTHER on their own in the re-run; a "Don't quote" row still counts as evidence. The user closed the thread with "Perfect, thanks Ahmed".

## 17. Session 6 (30 Sept 2026, later): SUST-REVENUE continued, Uganda hub KIIs

Continued SUST-REVENUE after section 16. Rows came from Ateker FM SM, BBS Radio (EO, HC, RC1, SM), Radio Pacis (SM, RC1, RC2, HC, EPC). All verdicts are in table 15.1 (rows after UG_KII_AtekerFM_SM_31Aug L48). **Last row reviewed: UG_KII_RadioPacis_EPC_28Aug L36** (recoded SUST-CONTINUE), then BBS SM L213 re-shown. **Next session: the user will start a different code** (which one not said). SUST-REVENUE is not finished: check the remaining pending count in the app.

### 17.1 Conventions settled for SUST (apply consistently)
- **Donor/FRI money for the programme is continuation, not revenue:** current FRI contribution with payer named → SUST-CONTINUE; the earlier, larger level → SUST-LAPSE (Ateker SM L65-L66, L74). Same logic as the Guide's "12,000 from the district".
- **Classify every SUST-REVENUE row:** advertising (businesses paying to be associated, Thomas 9 Sept), paid airtime (a client, incl. donor agencies like JICA, buying programme time), donation/grant (church, diocese, SIGNIS, diaspora, NURI/DANIDA). Always say station-level vs Green Leaf.
- **Station-level income is still coded SUST-REVENUE**, including changes in it (falls, seasonality, late payment by clients); SUST-LAPSE only for Green Leaf elements.
- **"Still on board / has not left", no money on the span → SUST-CONTINUE**, not another SUST-REVENUE (RC2 L58, EPC L36). Count each sponsorship once across all rows and respondents (Syova at Pacis appears on SM L24, L90, RC2 L58, L60, EPC L36, L39, L53-L55).
- **Split figures across Q&A pairs:** each answer to a different question keeps its own row and carries its own figure (BBS SM L196-L197 peak, L198-L199 low; Ateker SM L138 now, L140 before). Do not compute an annual figure the respondent declined to give.
- **Hypothetical sponsors or time pressure without a transaction** → not SUST-REVENUE (BBS EO L150 → FUNC-CONTENT).
- **What would survive without donor funding** (hypothetical) → SUST-RESTART, not SUST-CONTINUE (Ateker SM L132, BBS SM L181).
- **Station overhead** (UCC licence, NSSF, URA/EFRIS, station staff salaries) → COST-BOUNDARY; presenters going unpaid → COST-VOLUNTEER.
- **Realised prices** (talk shows, mentions actually sold) are a better basis than rate cards for valuing station-borne Green Leaf airtime (COST-INKIND at replacement cost). BBS SM L173-L174 suggests Green Leaf airs in the talk-show slot, the station's best-selling time.
- **A partner or sponsor describing its own payments** (EPC) is still evidence of the transaction → SUST-REVENUE.
- **Rows re-shown still pending with no note** (BBS SM L213): the first decision probably did not save; re-enter the same verdict and check L210-L212 too.

### 17.2 Findings worth carrying to analysis
- **Contradictions to record, not resolve:** BBS SM L154 "No" Green Leaf sponsorship vs his own L210-L213 (three sponsors), BBS HC L69-L70 and the frame's Masindi example; Ateker RC one-month Agro and More sponsorship vs Ateker SM L8 "no direct sponsor"; Ateker SM calls the sponsored segment "Digging Deep", the RC "Let's get farming"; RC2 L36 "right now we don't have any" vs RC2 L58 Syova "not left".
- **Name resolutions:** MADIFA = Masindi District Farmers Association (BBS SM L215), not MAAIF; ARUDIFA = Arua District Farmers Association (Pacis RC2 L65); "Jika" = JICA, rice seed production in West Nile (Pacis HC L80); "Signet" probably SIGNIS; NURI probably Danida-funded (one funding line with DANIDA). EPC L39 question names "green leaf magazine program Otita agrikica ni" as one programme (more support for Otita Agrikicani = Green Leaf).
- **Radio Pacis figures (respondent's own words, SM L24):** Syova Seeds 3.3m UGX/month × 3 months (~10m); DK Seed 800,000 UGX/month × 4 months; FRI retainer 2.7m per three months since 2025 (1.7 from ~2018, cut to 1.2, L22); cost per episode ~4m incl. ~2.3m airtime if charged (L88); station group revenue up to 120m/month across four stations, 90-140 range (L109, L113); church covers ~20%.
- **Ateker FM:** FRI contribution ~2.48m (per quarter?) now vs ~7m before; station advertising ~60m/year now vs >100m before; UCC licence ~7m/year; last three sales 1.5m, 1.2m, 1.1m.
- **BBS:** peak-season advertising 25-30m UGX/month, low ~15m; top shows 1-3m/week; three small Green Leaf(?) sponsors (Hongera Sana, Naseco, AB Aligwaawa).

## 18. Session 7 (1 Oct 2026): SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED

The user started SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED (all countries): **0 of 14 decided, 14 left**, 2 "safe" rows offered (do not bulk-accept until the rows have been read; only 14 rows, so review each).

### 18.0 Checks for SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED (Appendix A + earlier verdicts)
- **Direction matters:** an outside party approached the hub or station. The station or RC going out to find a sponsor is not revealed demand (Ateker RC L56-L57: RC approached Agro and More → SUST-REVENUE only).
- **Money or a concrete offer must be involved.** Expressed interest, promises or hypotheticals → SUST-DEMAND-STATED (district officials promising to budget, Ateker SM L62).
- **What is wanted:** to sponsor, buy airtime on, or redeploy a **Green Leaf type programme**. Ordinary station advertising or talk-show bookings with no Green Leaf link → SUST-REVENUE (station-level), not this code. Redeployment of the Green Leaf format by an outside payer (IJM at Ateker SM L48, if money confirmed) and AGRA/PIATA/CABI redeployments are the core cases.
- **Explicit absence is codeable** (frame example: no approach to sponsor Green Leaf at Volta Star since donor exit). Volta Star EPC L376 ("apart from Farm Radio and the FAO, nobody came") was flagged as a recorded absence.
- **Second codes:** the money actually received → SUST-REVENUE (count each deal once); a continuing sponsor → SUST-CONTINUE; the partner → FUNC-PARTNER.
- **Never report alongside stated demand without saying which is which.**

**Status 1 Oct (Progress page):** 440 of 2,912 rows reviewed (15%; Ghana 206/1,096, Uganda 234/1,816), 0 of 76 sessions closed, 3 of 5 active. Coverage: Ghana ACC 85%, ADOPT 15%, COST 35%, SUST 19%; Uganda ACC 95%, SUST 24%; every other family 0% (incl. REACH, CTX, FUNC, ECO). Josephine asked (WhatsApp, 10:55) if coding is done; she told Bernard a first draft would be shared by 2 Oct; core findings can be bullet points. The user drafted a reply asking for time, targeting noon 2 Oct for the codes behind the core findings (not confirmed sent).

## 19. Session 7 continued (1 Oct 2026): SUST-RESTART

SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED left part-way (12 rows decided per table 18.1, remaining count to check). SUST-REVENUE: 65/70 at last look, Radio Savannah EO L810-L816 and Volta Star EPC L293-L294 left Pending. SUST-RESTART: **0 of 22 decided**. Short notes from now on (1-2 sentences).

### 19.0 Checks for SUST-RESTART
- What it would take to restore a lapsed element (costs, conditions) and what would not need spending again (retained assets: synopsis bank, topic calendar, trained staff, equipment still working).
- Includes what would survive or what conditions apply if donor funding ends (hypothetical) (17.1: Ateker SM L132, BBS SM L181).
- Not current running costs (COST-CASH/COST-INKIND); not an element still running with a payer (SUST-CONTINUE); the loss itself is SUST-LAPSE; one-off start-up costs already incurred are COST-SETUP.
- Wishes for more money with no lapsed element or condition named are not restart; a demand on funders (FRI should liaise with MAAIF) may be ECO-POLICY or FUNC-GOV.

## 20. Session 7 continued (1 Oct 2026): SUST-OTHER

SUST-RESTART: about 22 rows decided (table 19.1). The last two in 19.1 (Garden City EPC L302, L332) were already SUST-OTHER rows. SUST-OTHER: **0 of 72 decided**.

### 20.0 Checks for SUST-OTHER
- OTHER is the last resort: try every SUST code first (CONTINUE needs a named payer; LAPSE a programme element lost; REVENUE money received; DEMAND-REVEALED an outside approach with money; DEMAND-STATED prices or interest without a transaction; RESTART conditions, retained assets, hypothetical loss without donor money), then other families (COST, FUNC-GOV, CTX-STATUS/HIST, ECO-POLICY, ECO-COMPARATOR).
- Accept SUST-OTHER only when the content is about sustainability and fits nothing else; give the reason, since every use is reviewed fortnightly. Recurring content → note it for a Frame amendment.
- Hypothetical loss or what would survive without donor funding → SUST-RESTART (17.1, 19.1).
- **Lead decision (1 Oct, user delegated it to Claude):** SUST-RESTART holds conditions, costs, retained assets and hypothetical losses for restoring **or keeping** a programme element after donor funding falls, plus the hub's or station's own plans for funding it without FRI (find partners, sponsored segment, marketing). Each such plan row's note starts "Strategy:" so the rows can be split out if a new code is adopted. Proposed on the Frame page as an amendment. Not SUST-RESTART: an element already lost (SUST-LAPSE, e.g. Garden City EPC L331-L332), a running element with a payer (SUST-CONTINUE), money received (SUST-REVENUE), a don't-know (Reject). Station-level income changes → SUST-REVENUE. Hub has no collective plan for the funding gap → FUNC-GOV (Garden City EPC L302).

## 21. Session 7 continued (1 Oct 2026): SUST-CONTINUE

SUST-OTHER left at about 35 of 72 decided (rest pending). SUST-CONTINUE: **0 of 57 decided**, 11 safe rows offered.

### 21.0 Checks for SUST-CONTINUE
- Two things on the span or its question: an element still running, and the payer named (government budget, station's own budget, another donor incl. FRI, sponsor, community, unpaid time). Code per element.
- Guide: "We have 12,000 from the district" = SUST-CONTINUE (money received, payer named), not COST-CASH. "Because we don't have sponsorship, we come live and they repeat" = SUST-LAPSE (nothing running with a named payer).
- 17.1: current FRI contribution → SUST-CONTINUE; the earlier, larger level → SUST-LAPSE. Sponsor "still on board, has not left", no money on span → SUST-CONTINUE.
- No payer named → not this code: element status only → CTX-STATUS; hypothetical continuation without donor money → SUST-RESTART ("Risk:"); money received by the station with a payer but not tied to keeping an element running → SUST-REVENUE.
- Unpaid time as the payer → SUST-CONTINUE; COST-VOLUNTEER as second code where an individual absorbs the cost (Volta Star frame example).
- Safe bulk-accept: only after ~20 rows reviewed individually, and untick Uganda-station-level or FRI rows to read them.

## 22. Session 8 (1 Oct 2026): SUST-DEMAND-STATED

SUST-CONTINUE left at about 50 of 57 decided (Garden City EPC L59 Pending, can likely be Accepted per L16). SUST-DEMAND-STATED: **0 of 47 decided**, 9 safe rows offered (do not bulk-accept until ~20 rows read individually).

### 22.0 Checks for SUST-DEMAND-STATED
- Hypothetical pricing, rate cards, quoted rates, expressed appetite or conditional interest, **no transaction**. Guide: a quoted airtime rate ("1,200, 1,300 per hour") = SUST-DEMAND-STATED, not COST-INKIND.
- **Whose interest:** a buyer's or potential payer's interest or price (Ateker SM L62-L63, district promised to budget → SUST-DEMAND-STATED). The hub's or station's own plan or pitch to funders → SUST-RESTART "Strategy:" (Garden City SM L213, Radio Savannah EO L788-L796 → COST-VOLUNTEER). The hub only hoping someone pays (Ateker HC L104-L105) → SUST-RESTART "Strategy:".
- **Prices refused or quoted by the station** count (Ateker SM L30 refused 100,000 → SUST-DEMAND-STATED; Ateker SM L28 5,000 per mention = rate). Realised sales (money in) → SUST-REVENUE; an outside approach with money or a concrete offer → SUST-DEMAND-REVEALED.
- Farmers wanting new access routes (TV version) → ACC-OTHER, not this code (Sakogu L25).
- "Don't know / ask marketing" → Reject (Radio Savannah SM L290-L292).
- Always state it is stated, not revealed, demand; say station-level vs Green Leaf; currency check (cedis rendered as dollars; UGX assumed in Uganda).

## 23. Session 8 continued (1 Oct 2026): SUST-LAPSE

SUST-DEMAND-STATED: all 47 rows given verdicts (table 22.1; check the app shows 47/47). SUST-LAPSE: **0 of 52 decided**, 16 safe rows offered (read ~20 first).

### 23.0 Checks for SUST-LAPSE
- An element (production, airtime payment, field work, hub meetings, extension participation, facilitation, a segment, a sponsor) that **has ceased or shrunk since support diminished, with what was lost**. Coded per element.
- Guide: "because we don't have sponsorship, we come live and they repeat" = SUST-LAPSE, not SUST-CONTINUE.
- **Already happened**, not hypothetical: what would be lost if FRI left → SUST-RESTART "Risk:"; support already ended and something is being lost → SUST-LAPSE (Garden City EPC L331-L332).
- Earlier, larger FRI level → SUST-LAPSE; current level with payer → SUST-CONTINUE (17.1). Sponsor that left (Ateker RC L67) → SUST-LAPSE.
- **Station-level income falls → SUST-REVENUE**, not this (17.1). Status with no link to reduced support → CTX-STATUS. Never existed at the hub → not coded.
- Also lapse: format dropped (Volta HC L787-L788), real value eroded (Pacis HC L82 stipend vs fuel).
- **Missed SUST-LAPSE rows flagged earlier (check they appear or add):** Radio Savannah RP L19; Ateker RC L13; Ateker SM L72, L74 (recoded), L110; Ateker SM L142 (programme quality); Garden City SM L230-L232; Garden City EPC L56-L57 (ESRF 2024), L81-L82, L331-L332 (recoded); Volta HC L787-L788; Volta EPC L291; Pacis SM L22, L70; Pacis RC1 L27, L41; Pacis RC2 L38, L48, L58 (ARUDIFA/NURI ended?); Pacis HC L82; BBS HC L72-L73.

## 25. Session 8 continued (1 Oct 2026): ACC-DIRECT resumed

The ACC-DIRECT page now shows the adopted rule in "Do not apply to" (first-heard answers → ACC-OTHER or ACC-SHARED; radio as one source → ECO-COMPARATOR; set owned or controlled stated anywhere in the interview + actual listening) and eight settled decisions (phone lines → ACC-OTHER; "random moment… heard it" → ACC-OTHER; "listening to Ateker for so long" → ACC-OTHER; station phone contacts → ACC-OTHER; communal meetings and radios → ECO-COMPARATOR; wife's radio she controls → ACC-SHARED; "both are mine… he doesn't limit me" → ACC-DIRECT, no GESI-ACCESS).

### 25.0 Checks for ACC-DIRECT (12.2 + section 16)
- Two things: a set the respondent owns or controls (stated anywhere in the interview) and actual listening to the programme. Set with no listening → Reject (Kyakamese_05 L31-L33).
- First-heard (B1) answers → ACC-OTHER; first heard through a person/group → ACC-SHARED; B1 line naming own device → ACC-DIRECT (Andifeku_06 L16).
- Radio as one source in the general sources question → ECO-COMPARATOR, Quantified No.
- Household radio the respondent decides on → ACC-DIRECT; spouse owns and controls → ACC-SHARED (+ ACC-BARRIER + GESI-ACCESS on the control line).
- B3 frequency answers and repeat ownership to a different probe → own ACC-DIRECT rows. Past own set + listened → ACC-DIRECT; current device its own row.
- Phone numbers held, Uliza used → ACC-OTHER. GESI-ACCESS only with a stated difference.
- **Missed ACC-DIRECT rows still to check (11.5, 12.5):** Komaret_01 L20; Andifeku_04 L19; Rondo_02 L20 start; Rondo_01 L19; Kahaara L65; Komaret_03 L20; Rupa_06 L20; Kyamugweri_01 L20; Labongo_02 L23; Bekwai_01 L12; Bekwai_04 L18; Golinga_02 L19.

## 26. Session 8 continued (1 Oct 2026): ECO-POLICY

ACC-DIRECT left part-way (6 verdicts in 25.1; check the remaining count). ECO-POLICY started (count not shown on the screenshot). ECO family now in both sets (section 16).

### 26.0 Checks for ECO-POLICY
- Alignment with, or absence from, **national extension or digital agriculture strategy**: named strategies or policies (e.g. PDM, the Parish Development Model; MAAIF/MoFA positions; decentralisation; extension policy), ministry positions, budget lines. The frame's own example is a MoFA regional office with no radio programming, no liaison and no radio budget line since decentralisation, so ministry and district-office positions count.
- Earlier calls carrying ECO-POLICY: Ateker HC L86 (district has no airtime allocation); Volta Star EPC L156-L160, L287 (no resources/funding at the department for radio); BBS EO L149 (MAAIF approves farmer content, FRI should liaise); Pacis HC L58 (MAAIF should close the funding gap); Ecosystem EPC2 L94 (district planning and budget integration); Garden City EPC L331 (MoFA never ran its own programme, support ended 2023).
- **Not ECO-POLICY:** hub-level decisions, agreements, disputes → FUNC-GOV; a district promising to budget → SUST-DEMAND-STATED; extension officers' participation and who pays → SUST-CONTINUE/LAPSE (ECO-POLICY only as second code when a policy or budget line is the point); face-to-face extension as an alternative channel → ECO-COMPARATOR; MADIFA/ARUDIFA are farmers' associations, not ministries.
- Government using the slot for its own programmes (vaccination, PDM mobilisation) → FUNC-PARTNER; ECO-POLICY where the programme is named as a channel for a national policy.

## 27. Session 8 continued (1 Oct 2026): DIFF-FORMAL

ECO-POLICY left part-way (21 verdicts in 26.1; check the remaining count). DIFF-FORMAL started (count not shown).

### 27.0 Checks for DIFF-FORMAL
- **Programme content** passes through a **standing group with membership and recurring meetings** (farmer association, cooperative, savings group/VSLA, a named listening group that meets). Named structure needed; ad hoc gatherings, neighbours, markets → DIFF-INFORMAL; household → DIFF-KIN.
- **Guide test against ACC-SHARED:** if the people reached had not heard it themselves → ACC-SHARED; if all had → DIFF. Where a group both relays to non-listeners and discusses among listeners, code both (Olevu L33: ACC-SHARED + DIFF-FORMAL). First heard about the programme through a group → ACC-SHARED (Andifeku_07 L16), not DIFF.
- Must be the programme's content, not farming information in general, and not the group's other business (savings, inputs, marketing → INC-MARKET / not coded).
- Flagged earlier as DIFF-FORMAL: Olevu L33 (village listening group), Olevu L42; Jamasi L36 (associations share tried practices at meetings); Jamasi L32 Adom Farmers Association (if content passes through it); Fodome Xelu L69-L70 (association meetings; the frame's example comes from this session).

## 28. Session 9 (2 Oct 2026): GESI-ACCESS

DIFF-FORMAL left part-way (21 verdicts in 27.1; Tilevu_02 rows on hold). GESI-ACCESS started (all countries): **0 of 47 decided**, 10 safe rows offered (read ~20 individually first). GESI is a both-set code (community and hub sessions).

### 28.0 Checks for GESI-ACCESS (Appendix A + earlier conventions)
- **A stated difference between groups** in access to the broadcast, inputs (land, capital, seed) or extension, with the access mechanism given. Outcome differences with no mechanism → not coded. "No difference" / "he doesn't limit me" → not coded (Kyamugweri_06 L37; absence rule still an open Frame question, 11.6).
- **Groups:** sex, age/youth (11.3, Olevu L39), disability, wealth or other named group. A single household's arrangement counts when the sex difference is the point (spouse controls the set: Kahaara L63 husband, Andifeku_07 L19 wife → ACC-BARRIER + GESI-ACCESS).
- **Owner controls the radio, no sex or group stated** → ACC-BARRIER only (Naitakwai L36).
- **Siblings:** a belief or convention about who may listen, speak, attend or adopt → GESI-NORM (personal bias is not a norm: Kahaara L42, Rupa_05 L75); logistical barriers stay GESI-ACCESS + ACC-BARRIER/ADOPT-BARRIER. Women's roles in the hub (WIAD/WIADO, Ghana only) → GESI-ROLE. A design commitment on inclusion missed in fieldwork (women-only FGD, gender balance) → GESI-GAP.
- **Second codes:** the barrier itself keeps its ACC-BARRIER (hearing) or ADOPT-BARRIER (acting) row; GESI-ACCESS records the group difference.
- **Hypothetical loss of women's/youth voices if funding falls** → SUST-RESTART "Risk:", not GESI-ACCESS (Pacis RC1 L33).
- **Flagged earlier as GESI-ACCESS (check they appear):** Kahaara L56 (wife, + ACC-SHARED), L63, L67 (women at home, men at trading centres at airtime); Olevu L36 (men have radios, women hear late); Olevu L39 (youth, accepted); Andifeku_07 L19 (wife controls); Sankpagla L51 (women's group relies on husbands' sets, lead to judge whether the difference is stated enough).
- **Holds:** Tilevu_02 rows (transcript integrity, 27.1); Garden City rows carry the Akuafo Mrre identity check.

## 29. Session 10 (8 Oct 2026): FUNC-CONTENT

GESI-ACCESS left at about 42 of 47 (4 Pending, see 28.1). FUNC-CONTENT started (count not shown). FUNC is a Hub-set code.

### 29.0 Checks for FUNC-CONTENT
- How topics are chosen, scripted, scheduled and prepared, and planning assets (topic calendars, synopses, content design documents, work plans), including resource-person briefing and matching.
- Not the broadcast slot (ACC-SCHED); not who sits on the hub (CTX-HUB, which can sit beside it on the same span: roster vs briefing); not how farmer responses are collected and acted on (FUNC-FEEDBACK, which can sit beside it when Uliza material is selected for air).
- Time for content inside the hour and the format's needs (farmer voices, success stories) → FUNC-CONTENT (BBS EO L150, 15.1).
- **Recommendations for a different delivery channel** (practical demonstrations, field days) are not content design: ECO-COMPARATOR holds them. Recommendations about the programme's own content or segment length stay FUNC-CONTENT (Pacis EO L89-L90).
- Outside organisations supplying content or training → FUNC-PARTNER as second code.

FUNC-FEEDBACK: all 25 remaining rows given verdicts (30.1), should read 50/50. Josephine chased on WhatsApp (draft due next day); the user replied with these figures. FUNC-GOV: **18 of 46 decided, 28 left** (all accepted so far).

### 31.0 Checks for FUNC-GOV
- Who decides, who is accountable, how disputes or resourcing gaps are resolved: reporting lines, decision rights, formal agreements (MOUs, contracts) or their absence, accountability mechanisms (monitors, reports to FRI).
- Not who does the work (CTX-HUB); not national policy (ECO-POLICY); a predicted dispute if funding ends → SUST-RESTART "Risk:" (Ateker SM L156, 24.1); a hub with no plan for the gap → FUNC-GOV (Garden City EPC L302).

## 33. Session 10 continued (8 Oct 2026): FUNC-PARTNER

FUNC-OTHER: all 12 remaining rows given verdicts (32.1), should read 21/21. FUNC-PARTNER: **16 of 71 decided, 55 left** (accepted 9, recoded 1, rejected 6); bulk accept off for Ghana (56% kept). Settled decision on the code page: an organisation named as carrying the programme = FUNC-PARTNER, not ECO-ACTOR; merely operating in the same space with no contribution = ECO-ACTOR.

### 33.0 Checks for FUNC-PARTNER
- Organisation outside the hub contributing content, staff, airtime, funding or reach; named ministries, district governments, NGOs, input dealers, sponsors, other stations.
- Hub members (incl. the extension officers a hub says make it up, e.g. Ateker HC L65) → CTX-HUB, not this code.
- Sponsor paying money → SUST-REVENUE, with FUNC-PARTNER as second code where it also contributes content (15.1).
